The\n    client also resolves each dataset's current revision, which is what is on disk -- the\n    release tables list the pre-revision paths, so resolving from them alone finds no\n    trials. The metadata that resolution needs is cached under one_cache/.rest, so a run\n    does not have to reach Alyx.\n\n    datadir: directory holding the one_cache/ folder.\n    Sets the globals `one` and `subset_csv`; returns nothing.\n    \"\"\"\n    global one\n    global subset_csv\n    cache = os.path.join(datadir, ONE_CACHE_DIRNAME)\n    tables = os.path.join(cache, RELEASE)\n    client = dict(base_url=BASE_URL, silent=True, cache_dir=cache, tables_dir=tables,\n                  mode='remote')\n    try:\n        # No password: passing one forces re-authentication over the network, while the\n        # token the fetch cached needs no connection.\n        one = ONE(**client)\n    except Exception:\n        one = ONE(password=PASSWORD, **client)\n    one.alyx.default_expiry = REST_EXPIRY\n    # One.load_cache reads the parquet tables where they are; the OneAlyx override would\n    # ask Alyx for a newer copy first. probabilityLeft is constant within a block, so a change of value starts a new block\n    block = (trials.probabilityLeft != trials.probabilityLeft.shift()).cumsum()\n\n    return pd.DataFrame({'choice': trials.choice.map(CHOICE),\n                         'prior': trials.probabilityLeft.map(PRIOR),\n                         'trial_in_block': trials.groupby(block).cumcount()})\n\n\ndef covered(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Trials whose window is spanned by this stream, allowing the one bin gap the reference allows.\"\"\"\n    start, stop = stim_on + T_START, stim_on + T_STOP\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, start, side='right')      # first sample inside the window\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, stop, side='left') - 1       # last sample inside the window\n\n    # a trial with no sample at all, or a gap at either edge, has no usable trace\n    inside = begin <= end\n    first = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(begin, 0, times.size - 1)], np.inf)\n    last = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(end, 0, times.size - 1)], -np.inf)\n    return inside & (np.abs(start - first) <= BIN) & (np.abs(stop - last) <= BIN)\n\n\ndef usable_trials(trials, wheel_times, camera_times):\n    \"\"\"Trials the paper would keep, that also have a full wheel and camera window.\"\"\"\n    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()\n    reaction = trials.firstMovement_times.to_numpy() - stim_on\n\n    keep = (reaction >= MIN_RT) & (reaction <= MAX_RT)          # NaN compares false, so those go too\n    keep &= trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\n    keep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n\n    return keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n\n\n# --- behavior ---\n\ndef window_slice(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Index range of each trial window, so a stream is sliced rather than rescanned per trial.\"\"\"\n    window_start = stim_on + T_START\n    window_stop = stim_on + T_STOP\n\n    # the samples are in time order, so each window is one contiguous range of them\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, window_start)\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, window_stop)\n\n    return begin, end\n\n\ndef trial_traces(times, values, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"A continuous trace resampled onto the same time grid for every trial.\"\"\"\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    traces = []\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        # one sample either side of the window, so the first and last bin centre are\n        # interpolated rather than clamped to the nearest sample inside it\n        first = max(begin[trial] - 1, 0)\n        last = end[trial] + 1\n\n        traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n\n    return np.array(traces)\n\n\ndef discretize(trace):\n    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n\n\n# --- neural ---\n\ndef load_neural(eid):\n    \"\"\"Spike times and good clusters of a session, merged over its probes.\"\"\"\n    times, units, regions = [], [], []\n    for pid, name in zip(*one.eid2pid(eid)):\n        loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=name)\n\n        # skip the md5 check, which rereads every spike file and draws its own progress bar\n        spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting(check_hash=False)\n\n        # a session can hold an insertion whose spike sorting was never released\n        if not clusters:\n            continue\n\n        clusters = loader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels)   # attaches metrics and histology\n\n        # `void` is the atlas label for a channel the histology placed outside the brain,\n        # so a unit there is not in a brain region at all\n        in_brain = brain_regions.acronym2acronym(\n            clusters['acronym'], mapping='Beryl') != 'void'\n\n        # spikes.clusters holds the row of the cluster table, so a per cluster flag\n        # can be indexed by it directly to say whether each spike survives\n        is_good = (clusters['label'] >= QC_LABEL) & in_brain\n        keep = is_good[spikes['clusters']]\n\n        # What variables in the raw data is `output` *wheel_speed* derived from?\n\ni. Three steps. The wheel is recorded only when it moves, so `SessionLoader` first interpolates the position onto an even 1000 Hz grid and differentiates it into a velocity, applying a 20 Hz Butterworth low pass while doing so; the speed is the absolute value of that velocity, in radians per second. That trace is then resampled onto the 100 bin centres of each trial. Finally it is cut into three classes at the 33rd and 67th percentile of the session, so the three classes are equally sized within a session.\n\nii. What `SessionLoader.load_wheel` does internally:\n```python\nself.wheel['position'], self.wheel['times'] = interpolate_position(\n    wheel_raw['timestamps'], wheel_raw['position'], freq=fs)\nself.wheel['velocity'], self.wheel['acceleration'] = velocity_filtered(\n    self.wheel['position'], fs=fs, corner_frequency=corner_frequency, order=order)\n```\n\nResampling onto the bin centres, then splitting into three:\n```python\ntraces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n```\n\n```python\nSPLIT = [100 / 3, 2 * 100 / 3]       # equal sized classes, from percentiles of each session's own trace\n\ndef discretize(trace):\n    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n```\n\niii. The interpolation and the filter are not choices, they are what the IBL documentation recommends for computing wheel velocity and what `SessionLoader` does by default, so the reference and this conversion get the same trace.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *wheel_speed* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. What variables in the raw data is `output` *whisker_motion_energy* derived from?\n\ni. The motion energy of a side camera, `<side>Camera.ROIMotionEnergy` with its frame times `_ibl_<side>Camera.times`. It is a single value per video frame, computed by IBL over a square covering the whisker pad, and is used as released. The left camera is taken when the session has it and the right otherwise.\n\nii. It is resampled onto the 100 bin centres of each trial and then cut into three classes at the 33rd and 67th percentile of the session, the same rule as the wheel.\n\nii. Resampling onto the bin centres, then splitting into three:\n```python\nwhisker = trial_traces(motion_energy.times, motion_energy.iloc[:, 1], stim_on)\n```\n\n```python\ndef discretize(trace):\n    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n```\n\niii. No additional processing.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *whisker_motion_energy* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The camera trace is sampled at the same 100 bin centres as the neural data, measured from the same stimulus onset, so the two share a time axis bin for bin.\n\nii. The camera evaluated at the bin centres:\n```python\ntraces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n```\n\niii. The camera frame times are on the same session clock as the neural data, so evaluating the trace at the bin centres is all the alignment needed.\n\n## 9. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. The API handles most of it. Missing data is dropped: A trial whose window is not spanned by the wheel or the camera is dropped; A session left with no unit or fewer than two trials is dropped.\n\nii. Skipping an unreleased probe:\n```python\n# a session can hold an insertion whose spike sorting was never released\nif not clusters:\n    continue\n```\n\nDropping a session with nothing usable data:\n```python\nif res['n_units'] == 0 or res['n_trials'] < 2:\n    print('skipped %s: %d trials, %d units'\n          % (res['session_id'][:8], res['n_trials'], res['n_units']), flush=True)\n    continue\n```\n\nThe coverage test for wheel and camera data:\n```python\ninside = begin <= end\nfirst = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(begin, 0, times.size - 1)], np.inf)\nlast = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(end, 0, times.size - 1)], -np.inf)\nreturn inside & (np.abs(start - first) <= BIN) & (np.abs(stop - last) <= BIN)\n```\n\niii. Mostly are missing data that we drop from the final results.\n\n## 10-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. Reading the spike sorting off disk, dominated by the two spike arrays of a probe that between them run to hundreds of megabytes.\n\nii. Reading the spike sorting, the one expensive call:\n```python\nspikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting(check_hash=False)\n```\n\niii. The cost is mainly file I/O.\n\n## 10-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. Two loops run once per trial, one resampling the behavioural traces and one binning the spikes. Both could be written as a single call over all trials at once, the binning by offsetting each spike's index by its trial and the resampling by building one query vector. Neither was, because each trial is a slice of a different part of the session and the loop keeps that obvious; they cost about 0.1 s a session between them, so there is nothing to gain.\n\nii. The two per trial loops:\n```python\nfor trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n    spike_time = times[begin[trial]:end[trial]] - onset      # time from stimulus onset\n```\n\n```python\nfor trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n    first = max(begin[trial] - 1, 0)\n```\n\niii. Not really. Nothing that can improve the code significantly.\n\n## 10-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. N/A\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 10-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. N/A\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 10-e. How is memory usage optimized?\n\ni. The neural array is cast to float32 and the outputs to int8, which is where nearly all the size is. Sessions are held one at a time in each worker rather than all at once, and only the two spike arrays the conversion needs are read, which keeps the peak of a worker at about 3 GB on a two probe session.\n\nii. Static targets are repeated over time so that they can coexist with the\\\\\\\\n+two dynamic targets in one output array.\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+The script is deliberately usable with both the full mounted release and the\\\\\\\\n+small grading cache. \\\\\\\\\\\\\"bwm_release.csv\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+SUBSET_CSV = ROOT / \\\\\\\\\\\\\"data\\\\\\\\\\\\\" / \\\\\\\\\\\\\"DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+DEFAULT_OUTPUT = ROOT / \\\\\\\\\\\\\"converted_data.pkl\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+BIN_SIZE_S = 0.020\\\\\\\\n+OFF_START_S = -0.5\\\\\\\\n+OFF_END_S = 1.5\\\\\\\\n+N_TIME = int(round((OFF_END_S - OFF_START_S) / BIN_SIZE_S))\\\\\\\\n+TIME_FROM_STIMULUS = (\\\\\\\\n+    OFF_START_S + np.arange(N_TIME, dtype=np.float32) * BIN_SIZE_S\\\\\\\\n+)\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def _ordered_unique(values):\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Return unique values without sorting or relying on set order.\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+    return list(dict.fromkeys(values))\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def load_release_table(max_sessions: int | None = None) -> pd.DataFrame:\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Load the first_header.count(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"-\\\\\\\\\\\\\") == 4:\\\\\\\\n+                allowed.add(first_header)\\\\\\\\n+        release = release[release[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"eid\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].astype(str).isin(eids)].copy()\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def trial_numbers_in_block(probability_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"One-indexed trial ordinal within each contiguous probability block.\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+    out = np.ones(len(probability_left), dtype=np.float32)\\\\\\\\n+    for i in range(1, len(out)):\\\\\\\\n+        if probability_left[i] == probability_left[i - 1]:\\\\\\\\n+            out[i] = out[i - 1] + 1.0\\\\\\\\n+    return out\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def valid_trial_mask(trials: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Implement the repository's load_trials_and_mask defaults.\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    In addition to the default required fields and 0.08--2.00 s reaction-time\\\\\\\\n+    interval, prepare_data passes max_trial_len=10 s.  No-choice trials are\\\\\\\\n+    excluded.  The initial unbiased block is retained.\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+    required = [\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"stimOn_times\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"choice\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"feedback_times\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"probabilityLeft\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"firstMovement_times\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"feedbackType\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"goCue_times\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n+    ]\\\\\\\\n+    missing = [name for name in required if name not in trials]\\\\\\\\n+    if missing:\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\"trial table is missing columns {missing}\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    vals = trials[required].to_numpy(dtype=float)\\\\\\\\n+    mask = np.all(np.isfinite(vals), axis=1)\\\\\\\\n+    reaction_time = (\\\\\\\\n+        trials[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"firstMovement_times\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\\\\\\\\n+        - ib < 2:\\\\\\\\n+            continue\\\\\\\\n+        local_t = sample_times[ib:ie]\\\\\\\\n+        local_v = sample_values[ib:ie]\\\\\\\\n+        if abs(begin - local_t[0]) > BIN_SIZE_S:\\\\\\\\n+            continue\\\\\\\\n+        if abs(end - local_t[-1]) > BIN_SIZE_S:\\\\\\\\n+            continue\\\\\\\\n+        query = stimulus_time + relative_endpoints\\\\\\\\n+        interp = np.interp(query, local_t, local_v)\\\\\\\\n+        if not np.all(np.isfinite(interp)):\\\\\\\\n+            continue\\\\\\\\n+        result[i] = interp.astype(np.float32)\\\\\\\\n+        good[i] = True\\\\\\\\n+    return result, good\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def load_behaviors(\\\\\\\\n+    one: ONE, eid: str, stimulus_times: np.ndarray\\\\\\\\n+) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, str]:\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Load repository-defined wheel speed and whisker-pad motion energy.\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+    loader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\\\\\\\\n+    loader.load_wheel()\\\\\\\\n+    wheel_times = loader.wheel[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"times\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\\\\\\\\n+    wheel_speed = np.abs(loader.wheel[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"velocity\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float))\\\\\\\\n+    wheel, wheel_good = interpolate_trials(wheel_times, wheel_speed, stimulus_times)\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    last_error = None\\\\\\\\n+    for view in (\\\\\\\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\\\\\\\"right\\\\\\\\\\\\\"):\\\\\\\\n+        try:\\\\\\\\n+            loader.load_motion_energy(views=[view])\\\\\\\\n+            key = f\\\\\\\\\\\\\"{view}Camera\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+            motion_df = loader.motion_energy[key]\\\\\\\\n+            motion, motion_good = interpolate_trials(\\\\\\\\n+                motion_df[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"times\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float),\\\\\\\\n+                motion_df[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"whiskerMotionEnergy\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float),\\\\\\\\n+                stimulus_times,\\\\\\\\n+            )\\\\\\\\n+            # The reference uses left whenever it can be loaded, with right as\\\\\\\\n+            # a fallback.  A wholly unusable left trace is treated as failure.\\\\\\\\n+            if np.any(motion_good):\\\\\\\\n+                return wheel, motion, wheel_good & motion_good, view\\\\\\\\n+        except Exception as exc:  # fallback is part of the reference loader\\\\\\\\n+            last_error = exc\\\\\\\\n+    raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\"no usable whisker motion-energy trace ({last_error})\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def discretize_tertiles(values: np.ndarray) -> tuple[np.ndarray, list[float]]:\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Convert a session's continuous behavior into low/middle/high tertiles.\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+    thresholds = np.quantile(values.reshape(-1), [1.0 / 3.0, 2.0 / 3.0])\\\\\\\\n+    if not np.all(np.isfinite(thresholds)):\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"non-finite tertile thresholds\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+    # np.digitize creates exactly the required ordered labels 0, 1, 2.\\\\\\\\n+    labels = np.digitize(values, thresholds, right=False).astype(np.int8)\\\\\\\\n+    return labels, [float(thresholds[0]), float(thresholds[1])]\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def bin_probe_spikes(\\\\\\\\n+    one: ONE,\\\\\\\\n+    eid: str,\\\\\\\\n+    pid: str,\\\\\\\\n+    probe_name: str,\\\\\\\\n+    stimulus_times: np.ndarray,\\\\\\\\n+) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Load one Kilosort probe and count every sorted cluster in 20 ms bins.\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+    loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=probe_name)\\\\\\\\n+    spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting()\\\\\\\\n+    cluster_table = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(\\\\\\\\n+        spikes, clusters, channels, compute_metrics=False\\\\\\\\n+    ).to_df()\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    spike_times = np.asarray(spikes[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"times\\\\\\\\\\\\\"], dtype=float)\\\\\\\\n+    spike_clusters = np.asarray(spikes[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"clusters\\\\\\\\\\\\\"], dtype=np.int64)\\\\\\\\n+    cluster_ids = np.unique(spike_clusters)\\\\\\\\n+    if not len(cluster_ids):\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\"probe {probe_name} has no spikes\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+    if cluster_ids.min() < 0 or cluster_ids.max() >= len(cluster_table):\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\"probe {probe_name} cluster indices do not match metadata\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    # Searchsorted maps arbitrary (though usually consecutive) Kilosort IDs to\\\\\\\\n+    # rows in the output.  All sorted clusters are retained; no QC label filter.\\\\\\\\n+    counts = np.zeros(\\\\\\\\n+        (len(stimulus_times), len(cluster_ids), N_TIME), dtype=np.uint16\\\\\\\\n+    )\\\\\\\\n+    for trial_i, stimulus_time in enumerate(stimulus_times):\\\\\\\\n+        begin = stimulus_time + OFF_START_S\\\\\\\\n+        end = stimulus_time + OFF_END_S\\\\\\\\n+        ib = np.searchsorted(spike_times, begin, side=\\\\\\\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+        ie = np.searchsorted(spike_times, end, side=\\\\\\\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+        if ie <= ib:\\\\\\\\n+            continue\\\\\\\\n+        local_time = spike_times[ib:ie]\\\\\\\\n+        bin_idx = np.floor((local_time - begin) / BIN_SIZE_S).astype(np.int64)\\\\\\\\n+        local_cluster = np.searchsorted(cluster_ids, spike_clusters[ib:ie])\\\\\\\\n+        in_range = (bin_idx >= 0) & (bin_idx < N_TIME)\\\\\\\\n+        flat = local_cluster[in_range] * N_TIME + bin_idx[in_range]\\\\\\\\n+        hist = np.bincount(flat, minlength=len(cluster_ids) * N_TIME)\\\\\\\\n+        if hist.max(initial=0) > np.iinfo(np.uint16).max:\\\\\\\\n+            raise OverflowError(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"20 ms spike count exceeds uint16 capacity\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+        counts[trial_i] = hist.reshape(len(cluster_ids), N_TIME)\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    regions = cluster_table.iloc[cluster_ids][\\\\\\\\\\\\\"acronym\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].fillna(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"void\\\\\\\\\\\\\").astype(str)\\\\\\\\n+    beryl = BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(regions.to_numpy(), mapping=\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Beryl\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+    return counts, np.asarray(beryl, dtype=str)\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def make_session(\\\\\\\\n+    one: ONE, eid_rows: pd.DataFrame\\\\\\\\n+) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], np.ndarray, dict]:\\\\\\\\n+    \\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"Convert one behavioral session and return trial arrays plus metadata.\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n+    eid = str(eid_rows.iloc[0][\\\\\\\\\\\\\"eid\\\\\\\\\\\\\"])\\\\\\\\n+    session_loader = ValueError(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"fewer than two source trials\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    block_number = trial_numbers_in_block(\\\\\\\\n+        trials[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"probabilityLeft\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\\\\\\\\n+    )\\\\\\\\n+    source_mask = valid_trial_mask(trials)\\\\\\\\n+    source_indices = np.flatnonzero(source_mask)\\\\\\\\n+    if len(source_indices) < 2:\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"fewer than two trials pass the paper's trial mask\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    stimulus_times = trials[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"stimOn_times\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)[source_indices]\\\\\\\\n+    wheel, motion, behavior_good, motion_view = load_behaviors(\\\\\\\\n+        one, eid, stimulus_times\\\\\\\\n+    )\\\\\\\\n+    source_indices = source_indices[behavior_good]\\\\\\\\n+    stimulus_times = stimulus_times[behavior_good]\\\\\\\\n+    wheel = wheel[behavior_good]\\\\\\\\n+    motion = motion[behavior_good]\\\\\\\\n+    if len(source_indices) < 2:\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"fewer than two trials have complete behavior coverage\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    wheel_class, wheel_thresholds = discretize_tertiles(wheel)\\\\\\\\n+    motion_class, motion_thresholds = discretize_tertiles(motion)\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    probe_counts = []\\\\\\\\n+    probe_regions = []\\\\\\\\n+    for row in eid_rows.itertuples(index=False):\\\\\\\\n+        counts, regions = bin_probe_spikes(\\\\\\\\n+            one, eid, str(row.pid), str(row.probe_name), stimulus_times\\\\\\\\n+        )\\\\\\\\n+        probe_counts.append(counts)\\\\\\\\n+        probe_regions.append(regions)\\\\\\\\n+    neural_3d = np.concatenate(probe_counts, axis=1).astype(np.float32)\\\\\\\\n+    region_labels = np.concatenate(probe_regions)\\\\\\\\n+    if neural_3d.shape[1] == 0:\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"session has no sorted clusters\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    choice_raw = trials[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"choice\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)[source_indices]\\\\\\\\n+    if not np.all(np.isin(choice_raw, [-1.0, 1.0])):\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"choice contains values other than -1 and +1\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+    choice = (choice_raw == 1.0).astype(np.int8)\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    prior_raw = trials[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"probabilityLeft\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)[source_indices]\\\\\\\\n+    prior = np.full(len(prior_raw), -1, dtype=np.int8)\\\\\\\\n+    for value, label in ((0.2, 0), (0.5, 1), (0.8, 2)):\\\\\\\\n+        prior[np.isclose(prior_raw, value)] = label\\\\\\\\n+    if np.any(prior < 0):\\\\\\\\n+        raise ValueError(f\\\\\\\\\\\\\"unexpected probabilityLeft values {np.unique(prior_raw)}\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    trial_in_block = block_number[source_indices]\\\\\\\\n+    neural = []\\\\\\\\n+    decoder_input = []\\\\\\\\n+    decoder_output = []\\\\\\\\n+    for i in range(len(source_indices)):\\\\\\\\n+        neural.append(np.ascontiguousarray(neural_3d[i]))\\\\\\\\n+        decoder_input.append(\\\\\\\\n+            np.vstack(\\\\\\\\n+                [TIME_FROM_STIMULUS, np.full(N_TIME, trial_in_block[i], np.float32)]\\\\\\\\n+            ).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\\\\\\\\n+        )\\\\\\\\n+        decoder_output.append(\\\\\\\\n+            np.vstack(\\\\\\\\n+                [\\\\\\\\n+                    np.full(N_TIME, choice[i], np.int8),\\\\\\\\n+                    np.full(N_TIME, prior[i], np.int8),\\\\\\\\n+                    wheel_class[i],\\\\\\\\n+                    motion_class[i],\\\\\\\\n+                ]\\\\\\\\n+            )\\\\\\\\n+        )\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    info = {\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"eid\\\\\\\\\\\\\": eid,\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"subject\\\\\\\\\\\\\": str(eid_rows.iloc[0][\\\\\\\\\\\\\"subject\\\\\\\\\\\\\"]),\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"date\\\\\\\\\\\\\": str(eid_rows.iloc[0][\\\\\\\\\\\\\"date\\\\\\\\\\\\\"]),\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"session_number\\\\\\\\\\\\\": int(eid_rows.iloc[0][\\\\\\\\\\\\\"session_number\\\\\\\\\\\\\"]),\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"probe_names\\\\\\\\\\\\\": [str(x) for x in eid_rows[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"probe_name\\\\\\\\\\\\\"]],\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"source_trial_count\\\\\\\\\\\\\": int(len(trials)),\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"retained_trial_count\\\\\\\\\\\\\": int(len(source_indices)),\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"retained_trial_indices\\\\\\\\\\\\\": source_indices.astype(int).tolist(),\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"motion_energy_camera\\\\\\\\\\\\\": motion_view,\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"wheel_speed_tertile_thresholds\\\\\\\\\\\\\": wheel_thresholds,\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"whisker_motion_energy_tertile_thresholds\\\\\\\\\\\\\": motion_thresholds,\\\\\\\\n+        \\\\\\\\\\\\\"n_neurons\\\\\\\\\\\\\": int(neural_3d.shape[1]),\\\\\\\\n+    }\\\\\\\\n+    return neural, decoder_input, decoder_output, region_labels, info\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+def convert(output_path: Path, max_sessions: int | None = None) -> dict:\\\\\\\\n+    release = load_release_table(max_sessions=max_sessions)\\\\\\\\n+    eids = _ordered_unique(release[\\\\\\\\\\\\\"eid\\\\\\\\\\\\\"].astype(str))\\\\\\\\n+    if not eids:\\\\\\\\n+        raise RuntimeError(\\\\\\\\\\\\\"No sessions selected from the release table\\\\\\\\\\\\\")\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+    # Omitting the password uses the staged Alyx token and cached REST replies.\\\\\\\\n+    # This is needed because revised ALF objects are not all represented in the\\\\\\\\n+    # old static Brainwidemap parquet table.\\\\\\\\n+    one = ONE(\\\\\\\\n+        base_url=\\\\\\\\\\\\\"https://openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org\\\\\\\\\\\\\",\\\\\\\\n+        silent=True,\\\\\\\\n+        cache_dir=str(CACHE_DIR),\\\\\\\\n+    )\\\\\\\\n+\\\\\\\\n+ Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \\n\\n## Decoder Task\\n\\nTemporally align based on stimulus onset\\n\\n### Decoder Inputs:\\n- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\\n- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\\n\\n### Decoder Outputs\\n- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\\n- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\\n- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\\n- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\\n\\n## Target Data Format\\n\\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\\n\\n```python\\ndata = {\\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\\n            ...\\n        ],\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\\n\\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\\n\\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\\n        ...\\n    ],\\n\\n    'metadata': {\\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\\n        ...\\n    }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### Key Format Requirements\\n\\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \\n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\\n\\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \\n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \\n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \\n\\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\\n\\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\\n\\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \\n\\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\\n\\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \\n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \\n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\\n\\n## Decoder Reference\\n\\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\\n- Optional arguments:\\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\\n\\n### Purpose\\n\\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \\n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \\n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \\n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\\n5. ibllib/.flake8\\n/app/code/ibllib/.gitignore\\n/app/code/ibllib/CHANGELOG.md\\n/app/code/ibllib/CITATION.cff\\n/app/code/ibllib/LICENSE\\n/app/code/ibllib/MANIFEST.in\\n/app/code/ibllib/README.md\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/CONTRIBUTING.md\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/README.md\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/__init__.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/core.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/ephys_plots.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/plot.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/plot_base.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/processing.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/singlecell.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/brainbox/video.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/examples/__init__.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/ibllib/__init__.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/ibllib/exceptions.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/ibllib/time.py\\n/app/code/ibllib/readthedocs.yml\\n/app/code/ibllib/requirements-analysis.txt\\n/app/code/ibllib/requirements.txt\\n/app/code/ibllib/ruff.toml\\n/app/code/ibllib/run_tests\\n/app/code/ibllib/run_tests.bat\\n/app/code/ibllib/setup.py\\n'}]\n\nSTEP 9\nOBS: [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\\nWall time 0.1 seconds\\nOutput:\\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'From \"Exploiting correlations across trials and behavioral sessions to improve neural decoding\"\\n\\nOur goal is to decode behavior y \\\\in R^P from spike-sorted, temporally binned spike counts. For each trial k, neural activity has size N x T, where N is the number of neurons and T the number of time steps. Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps. For each trial, we constructed X \\\\in R^{N x T}  by aggregating spike counts and used it to compute a decoder estimate of the true behavior y \\\\in R^P. y is constant within a trial (“per-trial decoding”); when P = T, y varies across time (“per-timestep decoding”).\\n\\nWe apply our models to 433 IBL sessions,17 covering 270 brain regions and four behavioral variables: choice, prior, wheel speed, and whisker motion energy.\\n\\n In the IBL experiments, mice rotate a wheel to indicate the location of a visual stimulus, defined as their choice (Figure 1A). For the first 90 trials, the stimulus appears randomly on either side with equal probability; in subsequent trials, it appears predominantly on one side in blocks. Mice adapt their behavior to these changing probabilities, allowing estimation of each mouse’s trial-by-trial prior belief (prior) about stimulus location. Additionally, wheel speed and whisker motion energy near the whisker pad are recorded. Whisker motion energy is computed as the mean absolute difference between adjacent video frames within a bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye. Choice and prior are static within a trial, while wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz. See STAR Methods sections “data processing” and “hyperparameter selection” for details.\\n\\nData processing\\n\\nIBL trial-aligned dataset\\n\\nFor choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset. For prior, we also align trials to the stimulus onset, including neural activity from 0.6 s to 0.1 s pre-onset. The prior represents the mice’s estimate of the stimulus side probability. We use the same decoding window as in the previous study,27 focusing on the period with minimal wheel movements. Within each trial, we segment neural activity into 50-ms non-overlapping time bins. For each time bin, we bin spike counts using all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5,77 from each session. For dynamic behaviors (wheel speed and whisker motion energy), we select the first movement onset as the alignment event, and decode the behavior starting at the alignment event and ending at 1 s after the alignment event. The neural activity within each trial is binned into non-overlapping 20 ms bins. For each time bin, we similarly bin spike counts using all neurons from each session.\\n\\nIBL trial-unaligned dataset\\n\\nWe focus only on wheel speed and whisker motion energy, as variables like choice and prior are only defined during experimental trials, which are periods when stimuli are presented and mice respond for rewards. Unlike the trial-aligned dataset, we do not align neural activity and behavior to a specific event onset. Instead, we segment the continuous Neuropixels recordings into 2-s snippets to construct the trial-unaligned dataset for each session. These snippets cover intervals both during and outside experimental trials, capturing the repetitive, structured behaviors within trials as well as the more spontaneous behaviors outside of trials.\\n\\nFrom \"A brain-wide map of neural activity during complex behaviour\"\\n\\nWe trained 139 mice (94 male and 45 female) on the International Brain Laboratory (IBL) decision-making task (Fig. 1a,b). On each trial, a visual stimulus appeared to the left or right on a screen, and the mouse had to move it to the centre by turning a wheel with its front paws within 60\\u2009s (Fig. 1c). After an initial 90 unbiased trials, the prior probability for the stimulus to appear on the left or right side was constant over a block of trials at a ratio of 20:80% (right block) or 80:20% (left block). Blocks lasted for between 20 and 100 trials, which were drawn from a truncated geometric distribution (empirical mean of 51 trials). Block changes were not cued. Stimulus contrast was uniformly sampled from 5 possible values (100, 25, 12.5, 6.25 and 0%). The 0% contrast trials, when no stimulus was presented, were assigned to a left or right side following the probability distribution of the block. This allowed mice to perform above chance by incorporating this prior in their choices. Following a wheel turn, mice received positive feedback in the form of a water reward, or negative feedback in the form of a white-noise pulse and a 2-s time out. The next trial began after a delay, followed by a quiescence period during which the mice had to hold the wheel still.\\n\\nRecordings\\n\\nIn these mice, we inserted 699 Neuropixels probes (see an example of one recording of three trials in Fig. 1i), following a grid that covered the left hemisphere of the forebrain and midbrain, which typically represent stimuli or actions on the contralateral side, and the right hemisphere of the cerebellum and hindbrain, which typically represent the ipsilateral side (Fig. 2a). Recordings were collected by 12 laboratories in Europe and the USA, with most recordings using 2 simultaneous probe insertions. To ensure reproducibility, one brain location was targeted in every mouse in every laboratory, as described elsewhere25. Only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained for further analyses. Data were uploaded to a central server, preprocessed and shared through a standardized interface. To perform spike sorting on the recordings, we used a version of Kilosort with custom additions. This process produced 621,733\\u2009units (including multineuron activity), averaging 889 per probe. To separate individual neurons from clusters of multineuron activity, we then applied stringent quality-control metrics (based on those described in ref. 15), which identified 75,708 well-isolated neurons, averaging 108 per probe.\\n\\nMethods\\n\\nn brief, at the beginning of each trial, the mouse was required to not move the wheel for a quiescence period of 400–700\\u2009ms. After the quiescence period, a visual stimulus (Gabor patch) appeared on either the left or right (±35° azimuth) of the screen, with a contrast randomly sampled from a predefined set (100, 25, 12.5, 6 or 0%). A 100-ms tone (5-kHz sine wave) was played at stimulus onset. Mice had 60\\u2009s to move the wheel and make a response. Stimuli were yoked to the rotation of the response wheel, such that a 1-mm movement of the wheel moved the stimulus by 4 visual degrees. A response was registered if the centre of the stimulus crossed the  ±35° azimuth line from its original position. If the mouse correctly moved the stimulus 35° to the centre of the screen, it immediately received a 3-μl reward; if it incorrectly moved the stimulus 35° away from the centre, it received a time out. If the mouse responded incorrectly or failed to reach either threshold within the 60-s window, a white-noise burst was played for 500\\u2009ms and the inter-trial interval was between 1 and 1.5 s. In trials for which the visual stimulus contrast was set to 0%, the mouse had to respond as for any other trial by turning the wheel in the correct direction (assigned according to the statistics of the prevailing block) to receive a reward, but the mouse was not able to perceive whether the stimulus was presented on the left or right side of the screen. The mouse also received feedback (noise burst or reward) on 0% contrast trials.\\n\\nEach session started with 90 trials in which the probability of a visual stimulus appearing on the left or right side was equal. Specifically, the 100%, 25%, 12.5% and 6% contrast trials were each presented 10 times on each side, and the 0% contrast was presented 10 times in total (that is, the ratio of the 100, 25, 12.5, 6 and 0% contrasts were set at 2, 2, 2, 2 and 1, respectively). The side (and thus correct movement) for the 0% contrast trials was chosen randomly between the right and left with equal probability. This initial block of 90 trials is referred to as the unbiased block (50:50).\\n\\nAfter the unbiased block, trials were presented in biased blocks: in right-bias blocks, stimuli appeared on the right on 80% of the trials, whereas in left-bias blocks, stimuli appeared on the right on 20% of the trials. The ratio of the contrasts remained as above (2:2:2:2:1). Whether the first biased block in a session was left or right was randomly chosen, and blocks were then alternated. The length of a block was drawn from an exponential distribution with scale parameter of 60 trials, but truncated to lie between 20 and 100 trials.\\n\\nThe automated shaping protocol for training involved two collections of sessions. In the first session, the animals started performing a version of the task without biased blocks and were then progressively introduced to harder stimuli with weaker contrasts as they became progressively more competent. They also experienced a debiasing protocol, which was intended to dissuade them from persisting with just one of the choices. Once they were performing sufficiently well on all non-zero contrasts, they were faced with the biased blocks. When, in turn, performance on those was adequate (including on 0% contrast trials, which are informed by the block), they graduated to recording. Supplementary Figure 8a shows a joint histogram of the number of sessions the mice took in the first and second collections (these were not correlated). Supplementary Figure 8b shows a joint histogram of the number of sessions the mice took in the second collection and the performance during the recording sessions. These were also not correlated.\\n\\nInclusion criteria\\n\\nWe applied a set of inclusion criteria to sessions, probes and neurons to ensure data quality. Supplementary Table 1 lists the consequences of these criteria for the number of sessions and probes that passed the criteria.\\nSessions and insertions\\n\\nEach Neuropixels insertion was repeated in at least two laboratories, with reproducibility of outcomes across laboratories verified with extensive analyses that we have previously reported25.\\n\\nSessions were included in the data release if the mice performed at least 250 trials, with a performance of at least 90% correct on 100% contrast trials for both left and right blocks, and, to be able to analyse the feedback variable, if there were at least 3\\u2009trials with incorrect choices (after applying the trial exclusions below). Furthermore, sessions were included in the release only if they reached threshold on a collection of hardware tests (definitions are available from GiHub (https://int-brain-lab.github.io/iblenv/_autosummary/ibllib.qc.task_metrics.html)).\\n\\nInsertions were excluded if the neural data failed the whole recording per visually assessed criteria of the ‘Recording Inclusion metrics and Guidelines for Optimal Reproducibility’ (RIGOR) from ref. 25, by presenting major artefacts (see examples in ref. 28) or if the probe tract could not be recovered during the histology procedure. Furthermore, only insertions for which alignments had been resolved (see appendix\\u20096 of ref. 25 for definitions) were used in this study.\\n\\nAfter applying these criteria, a total of 459 sessions, 699 insertions and 621,733 neurons remained, constituting the publicly released dataset.\\nTrials\\n\\nFor the analyses presented here, trials were excluded if one of the following trial events could not be detected: choice, probabilityLeft, feebackType, feeback times, stimON times and firstMovement times. Trials were further excluded if the time between stimulus onset and the first movement of the wheel (the first wheel-movement time) were outside the range of 0.08–2.00\\u2009s.\\nNeurons and brain regions\\n\\nNeurons generated by the spike-sorting pipeline were excluded from the analyses presented here if they failed one of the three criteria described in ref. 28 (the single unit computed metrics of RIGOR25): amplitude\\u2009>\\u200950\\u2009μV; noise cut-off\\u2009<\\u200920\\u2009μV; and refractory period violation. Neurons that passed these criteria were termed well-isolated neurons (or often just ‘neurons’) in this study. Out of the 621,733 units collected, 75,708 were considered well-isolated neurons. Final analyses were additionally restricted to regions that were designated grey matter in the adult mouse Allen Common Coordinate framework6, contained at least five well-isolated neurons per session and were recorded from in at least two such sessions.\\n\\nVideo analysis\\n\\nWe briefly describe the video analysis pipeline (full details can be found in ref. 105). The recording rigs contained three cameras: one called ‘left’ at full resolution (1,280\\u2009×\\u20091,024) and 60\\u2009Hz, filming the mouse from one side; one called ‘right’, filming the mouse symmetrically from the other side at half resolution (640\\u2009×\\u2009512) and 150\\u2009Hz; and one called ‘body’ at half resolution and 30\\u2009Hz, filming the body of the mouse from above. We developed several quality-control metrics to detect raw video issues such as poor illumination (as infrared light bulbs broke) or accidental misplacement of the cameras105.\\n\\nWe computed the motion energy (the mean across pixels of the absolute value of the difference between adjacent frames) of the whisker pad areas in the ‘left’ and ‘right’ videos (Fig. 1d). The whisker pad area was empirically defined using a rectangular bounding box anchored between the nose tip and the eye, both found using DeepLabCut106 (DLC; see more below). This metric quantifies motion in the whisker pad area and has a temporal resolution of the respective camera.\\n\\nWe also performed markerless pose estimation of body parts using DLC31, which is used in a fully automated pipeline in IBL (v.2.1) to track various body parts such as the paws, nose, tongue and pupil (Fig. 1d). In all analyses using DLC estimates, we excluded predictions with likelihood\\u2009<\\u20090.9. Furthermore, we developed several quality-control metrics for the DLC traces105.\\n\\nOverview of decoding\\n\\nWe performed a decoding analysis to measure how much information the activity of populations of neurons contained about task variables such as stimulus side and choice. To do this we, used cross-validated, maximum-likelihood regression with L1 regularization (to zero out the contribution from noisy neurons). The neural regressors were defined by binning the spike counts from each neuron in each session in a given region in a specific time window on each trial. The duration of the time window, the number of bins in that time window (that is, the bin size) and the trial event to which it was aligned depended on the variable that is the target of our regression (Supplementary Table 2). These factors are discussed further below and include a variety of behavioural and task variables: stimulus side, choice, feedback and wheel speed and velocity. Although a session may have included multiple probe insertions, we did not perform decoding on these probes separately because they are not independent. Instead, neurons in the same session and region were combined across probes for our decoding analysis. Decoding was cross-validated and compared with a null distribution to test for significance. A given region may have been recorded on multiple sessions; therefore, in the main figures (Figs. 4–7) the region P\\u2009value was defined by combining session P\\u2009values using Fisher’s combined probability test, and the region effect size was defined by subtracting the median of the null distribution from the decoding score and reporting the median of the resulting values across sessions. The P\\u2009values for all regions were then subjected to FDR correction for multiple comparisons at q\\u2009=\\u20090.01.\\n\\nDecoding target variables\\n\\nStimulus side, choice and feedback were treated as binary target variables for logistic regression. For stimulus side, trials that had zero contrast were excluded. We used the LogisticRegression module from scikit-learn111 (v.1.1.2) with 0.001 tolerance, 20,000 maximum iterations, “l1” penalty, “liblinear” solver and “fit_intercept” set to True. We balanced decoder classes by weighting samples by the inverse of the class frequency, 1/(2Pi,class). Decoding performance was evaluated using the balanced accuracy of classification, which is the average of the recall probabilities for the two classes. Supplementary Figure 9 shows histograms of the regression coefficients for all the variables.\\n\\nWheel values (speed and velocity) change over the course of a trial, unlike the previous decoding targets, and we therefore had to treat these target variables differently. We averaged wheel values in nonoverlapping 20-ms bins, starting 200\\u2009ms before first wheel-movement time and ending at 1,000\\u2009ms after first wheel-movement time. Spike counts were similarly binned. The target value for a given bin (ending at time t) was decoded from spikes in a preceding (causal) window spanning W bins (ending at times t, …, t-W). Therefore, if decoding from n neurons, there were (W\\u2009+\\u20091)n predictors of the target variable in a given bin. In practice, we used W\\u2009=\\u200910. To decode these continuous-valued targets, we performed linear regression using the Lasso module from scikit-learn111 (v.1.1.2) with 0.001 tolerance, 1,000 maximum iterations and “fit_intercept” set to True. Decoding performance was evaluated using the R2 metric.\\n\\n--- README ---\\n## Exploiting statistical structure in the data to improve neural decoding\\n\\n<p align=\"center\">\\n    <img src=assets/figure.jpg />\\n</p>\\n\\n### Environment setup\\n\\nCreate conda environment:\\n```\\nconda env create -f env.yaml\\n```\\n\\nActivate the environment:\\n```\\nconda activate that may or may not pass certain quality metrics.\\n\\n    Parameters\\n    ----------\\n    one: one.api.ONE\\n        Instance to be used to connect to local or remote database\\n    pid: str\\n        A probe insertion UUID\\n    compute_metrics: bool\\n        If True, force SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters to recompute the cluster metrics. If use good clusters, set qc to 1.\\n    kwargs:\\n        Keyword arguments passed to SpikeSortingLoader upon initiation. Specifically, if one instance offline,\\n        you need to pass \\'eid\\' and \\'pname\\' here as they cannot be inferred from pid in offline mode.\\n\\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n    selected_spikes: dict\\n        Spike trains associated with clusters. Dictionary with keys [\\'depths\\', \\'times\\', \\'clusters\\', \\'amps\\']\\n    selected_clusters: pandas.DataFrame\\n        Information of clusters for this pid \\n    sampling_freq: float\\n        Sampling frequency of spiking data (action potential)\\n    \"\"\"\\n    eid = kwargs.pop(\\'eid\\', \\'\\')\\n    pname = kwargs.pop(\\'pname\\', \\'\\')\\n    spike_loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=pname)\\n    sampling_freq = spike_loader.raw_electrophysiology(band=\"ap\", stream=True).fs\\n    \\n    spikes, clusters, channels = spike_loader.load_spike_sorting()\\n    clusters_labeled = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(\\n        spikes, clusters, channels, compute_metrics=compute_metrics).to_df()\\n    if qc is None:\\n        return spikes, clusters_labeled, sampling_freq\\n    else:\\n        iok = clusters_labeled[\\'label\\'] >= qc\\n        selected_clusters = clusters_labeled[iok]\\n        spike_idx, ib = ismember(spikes[\\'clusters\\'], selected_clusters.index)\\n        selected_clusters.reset_index(drop=True, inplace=True)\\n        selected_spikes = {k: v[spike_idx] for k, v in spikes.items()}\\n        selected_spikes[\\'clusters\\'] = selected_clusters.index[ib].astype(np.int32)\\n        return selected_spikes, selected_clusters, sampling_freq\\n\\n\\ndef merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Merge spikes and clusters information from several probes as if they were recorded from the same probe.\\n    This can be used to account for the fact that data from the probes recorded in the same session are not\\n    statistically independent as they have the same underlying behaviour.\\n\\n    NOTE: The clusters dataframe will be re-indexed to avoid duplicated indices. Accordingly, spikes[\\'clusters\\']\\n    will be updated. To unambiguously identify clusters use the column \\'uuids\\'\\n\\n    Parameters\\n    ----------\\n    spikes_list: list of dicts\\n        List of spike dictionaries as loaded by SpikeSortingLoader or brainwidemap.load_good_units\\n    clusters_list: list of pandas.DataFrames\\n        List of cluster dataframes as loaded by SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters or brainwidemap.load_good_units\\n\\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n    merged_spikes: dict\\n        Merged and time-sorted spikes in single dictionary, where \\'clusters\\' is adjusted to index into merged_clusters\\n    merged_clusters: pandas.DataFrame\\n        Merged clusters in single dataframe, re-indexed to avoid duplicate indices.\\n        To unambiguously identify clusters use the column \\'uuids\\'\\n    \"\"\"\\n\\n    assert (len(clusters_list) == len(spikes_list)), \\'clusters_list and spikes_list must have the same length\\'\\n    assert all([isinstance(s, dict) for s in spikes_list]), \\'spikes_list must contain only dictionaries\\'\\n    assert all([isinstance(c, pd.DataFrame) for c in clusters_list]), \\'clusters_list must contain only pd.DataFrames\\'\\n\\n    merged_spikes = []\\n    merged_clusters = []\\n    cluster_max = 0\\n\\n    for clusters, spikes in zip(clusters_list, spikes_list):\\n        spikes[\\'clusters\\'] += cluster_max\\n        cluster_max = clusters.index.max() + 1\\n        merged_spikes.append(spikes)\\n        merged_clusters.append(clusters)\\n        \\n    merged_clusters = pd.concat(merged_clusters, ignore_index=True)\\n    merged_spikes = {k: np.concatenate([s[k] for s in merged_spikes]) for k in merged_spikes[0].keys()}\\n    # Sort spikes by spike time\\n    sort_idx = np.argsort(merged_spikes[\\'times\\'], kind=\\'stable\\')\\n    merged_spikes = {k: v[sort_idx] for k, v in merged_spikes.items()}\\n\\n    return merged_spikes, merged_clusters\\n\\n\\ndef load_trials_and_mask(\\n        one, eid, min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2., nan_exclude=\\'default\\', min_trial_len=None,\\n        max_trial_len=None, exclude_unbiased=False, exclude_nochoice=True, sess_loader=None):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Function to load all trials for a given session and create a mask to exclude all trials that have a reaction time\\n    shorter than min_rt or longer than max_rt or that have NaN for one of the specified events.\\n\\n    Parameters\\n    ----------\\n    one: one.api.ONE\\n        Instance to be used to connect to local or remote database\\n    eid: str\\n        A session UUID\\n    min_rt: float or None\\n        Minimum admissible reaction time in seconds for a trial to be included. Default is 0.08. If None, don\\'t apply.\\n    max_rt: float or None\\n        Maximum admissible reaction time in seconds for a trial to be included. Default is 2. If None, don\\'t apply.\\n    nan_exclude: list or \\'default\\'\\n        List of trial events that cannot be NaN for a trial to be included. If set to \\'default\\' the list is\\n        [\\'stimOn_times\\',\\'choice\\',\\'feedback_times\\',\\'probabilityLeft\\',\\'firstMovement_times\\',\\'feedbackType\\']\\n    min_trial_len: float or None\\n        Minimum admissible trial length measured by goCue_time (start) and feedback_time (end).\\n        Default is None.\\n    max_trial_len: float or Nona\\n        Maximum admissible trial length measured by goCue_time (start) and feedback_time (end).\\n        Default is None.\\n    exclude_unbiased: bool\\n        True to exclude trials that fall within the unbiased block at the beginning of session.\\n        Default is False.\\n    exclude_nochoice: bool\\n        True to exclude trials where the animal does not respond. Default is False.\\n    sess_loader: brainbox.io.one.SessionLoader or NoneType\\n        Optional SessionLoader object; if None, this object will be created internally\\n\\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n    trials: pandas.DataFrame\\n        Trials table containing all trials for this session. If complete with columns:\\n        [\\'stimOff_times\\',\\'goCueTrigger_times\\',\\'feedbackType\\',\\'contrastLeft\\',\\'contrastRight\\',\\'rewardVolume\\',\\n        \\'goCue_times\\',\\'choice\\',\\'feedback_times\\',\\'stimOn_times\\',\\'response_times\\',\\'firstMovement_times\\',\\n        \\'probabilityLeft\\', \\'intervals_0\\', \\'intervals_1\\']\\n    mask: pandas.Series\\n        Boolean Series to mask trials table for trials that pass specified criteria. True for all trials that should be\\n        included, False for all trials that should be excluded.\\n    \"\"\"\\n\\n    if nan_exclude == \\'default\\':\\n        nan_exclude = [\\n            \\'stimOn_times\\',\\n            \\'choice\\',\\n            \\'feedback_times\\',\\n            \\'probabilityLeft\\',\\n            \\'firstMovement_times\\',\\n            \\'feedbackType\\'\\n        ]\\n\\n    if sess_loader is None:\\n        sess_loader = SessionLoader(one, eid)\\n\\n    if sess_loader.trials.empty:\\n        sess_loader.load_trials()\\n\\n    # Create a mask for trials to exclude\\n    # Remove trials that are outside the allowed reaction time range\\n    if min_rt is not None:\\n        query = f\\'(firstMovement_times - stimOn_times < {min_rt})\\'\\n    else:\\n        query = \\'\\'\\n    if max_rt is not None:\\n        query += f\\' | (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times > {max_rt})\\'\\n    # Remove trials that are outside the allowed trial duration range\\n    if min_trial_len is not None:\\n        query += f\\' | (feedback_times - goCue_times < {min_trial_len})\\'\\n    if max_trial_len is not None:\\n        query += f\\' | (feedback_times - goCue_times > {max_trial_len})\\'\\n    # Remove trials with nan in specified events\\n    for event in nan_exclude:\\n        query += f\\' | {event}.isnull()\\'\\n    # Remove trials in unbiased block at beginning\\n    if exclude_unbiased:\\n        query += \\' | (probabilityLeft == 0.5)\\'\\n    # Remove trials where animal does not respond\\n    if exclude_nochoice:\\n        query += \\' | (choice == 0)\\'\\n    # If min_rt was None we have to clean up the string\\n    if min_rt is None:\\n        query = query[3:]\\n\\n    # Create mask\\n    mask = ~sess_loader.trials.eval(query)\\n\\n    return sess_loader.trials, mask\\n\\n\\ndef list_brain_regions(neural_dict, **kwargs):\\n    brainreg = BrainRegions()\\n    beryl_reg = brainreg.acronym2acronym(neural_dict[\\'cluster_regions\\'], mapping=\\'Beryl\\')\\n    regions = ([[k] for k in np.unique(beryl_reg)] if kwargs[\\'single_region\\'] else [np.unique(beryl_reg)])\\n    print(f\"Use spikes from brain regions: \", regions[0])\\n    return regions, beryl_reg\\n\\n\\ndef select_brain_regions(regressors, beryl_reg, region, **kwargs):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Select units based on brain region.\\n    \"\"\"\\n    reg_mask = np.isin(beryl_reg, region)\\n    reg_clu_ids = np.argwhere(reg_mask).flatten()\\n    return reg_clu_ids\\n\\n\\ndef create_intervals(start_time, end_time, interval_len):\\n    interval_begs = np.arange(\\n        start_time, end_time-interval_len, interval_len\\n    )\\n    interval_ends = np.arange(\\n        start_time+interval_len, end_time, interval_len\\n    )\\n    return np.c_[interval_begs, interval_ends]\\n\\n\\ndef get_spike_data_per_interval(times, clusters, interval_begs, interval_ends, interval_len, binsize, n_workers=os.cpu_count()):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Select spiking data for specified interval in each recording.\\n\\n    Parameters\\n    ----------\\n    times : array-like\\n        time in seconds for each spike\\n    clusters : array-like\\n        cluster id for each spike\\n    interval_begs : array-like\\n        beginning of each interval in seconds\\n    interval_ends : array-like\\n        end of each interval in seconds\\n    interval_len : float\\n    binsize : float\\n        width of each bin in seconds\\n\\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n    tuple\\n        - (list): time in seconds for each interval; timepoints refer to the start/left edge of a bin\\n        - (list): data for each interval of shape (n_clusters, n_bins)\\n\\n    \"\"\"\\n    n_intervals = len(interval_begs)\\n\\n    # np.ceil because we want to make sure our bins contain all data\\n    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))\\n\\n    cluster_ids = np.unique(clusters)\\n    n_clusters_in_region = len(cluster_ids)\\n\\n    # This allows multiprocessing to work with nested functions\\n    @globalize\\n    def compute_spike_count(interval):\\n        # We use interval_idx to track the interval order while working with p.imap_unordered()\\n        interval_idx, t_beg, t_end = interval\\n        idxs_t = (times >= t_beg) & (times < t_end)\\n        times_curr = times[idxs_t]\\n        clust_curr = clusters[idxs_t]\\n        if times_curr.shape[0] == 0:\\n            # no spikes in this trial\\n            binned_spikes_tmp = np.zeros((n_clusters_in_region, n_bins))\\n            if np.isnan(t_beg) or np.isnan(t_end):\\n                t_idxs = np.nan * np.ones(n_bins)\\n            else:\\n                t_idxs = np.arange(t_beg, t_end + binsize / 2, binsize)\\n            idxs_tmp = np.arange(n_clusters_in_region)\\n        else:\\n            # bin spikes\\n            binned_spikes_tmp, t_idxs, cluster_idxs = bincount2D(\\n                times_curr, clust_curr, xbin=binsize, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])\\n            # find indices of clusters that returned spikes for this trial\\n            _, idxs_tmp, _ = np.intersect1d(cluster_ids, cluster_idxs, return_indices=True)\\n        return binned_spikes_tmp[:, :n_bins], idxs_tmp, interval_idx\\n\\n    binned_spikes = np.zeros((n_intervals, n_clusters_in_region, n_bins))\\n    with multiprocessing.Pool(processes=n_workers) as p:\\n        intervals = list(zip(np.arange(n_intervals), interval_begs, interval_ends))\\n        with tqdm(total=len(intervals)) as pbar:\\n            for res in p.imap_unordered(compute_spike_count, intervals):\\n                pbar.update()\\n                binned_spikes[res[-1], res[1], :] += res[0]\\n        pbar.close()\\n        p.close()\\n    return binned_spikes\\n\\n\\ndef bin_spiking_data(reg_clu_ids, neural_df, intervals=None, trials_df=None, n_workers=os.cpu_count(), **kwargs):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Format a single session-wide array of spikes into a list of trial-based arrays.\\n    The ordering of clusters used in the output are also returned.\\n\\n    Parameters\\n    ----------\\n    reg_clu_ids : array-like\\n        array of cluster ids for each spike\\n    neural_df : pd.DataFrame\\n        keys: \\'spike_times\\', \\'spike_clusters\\', \\'cluster_regions\\', \\'cluster_qc\\', \\'cluster_df\\'\\n    intervals : \\n        array of time intervals for each recording chunk including trials and non-trials\\n    trials_df : pd.DataFrame\\n        columns: \\'choice\\', \\'feedback\\', \\'pLeft\\', \\'firstMovement_times\\', \\'stimOn_times\\',\\n        \\'feedback_times\\'\\n    kwargs\\n        align_time : str\\n            event in trial on which to align intervals\\n            \\'firstMovement_times\\' | \\'stimOn_times\\' | \\'feedback_times\\'\\n        time_window : tuple\\n            (window_start, window_end), relative to align_time\\n        binsize : float, optional\\n            size of bins in seconds for multi-bin decoding\\n            \\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n    list\\n        each element is a 2D numpy.ndarray for a single interval of shape (n_bins, n_clusters)\\n    array\\n        cluster ids that account for axis 1 of the above 2D arrays.\\n    \"\"\"\\n\\n    if trials_df is not None:\\n        # compute time intervals for each trial\\n        intervals = np.vstack([\\n            trials_df[kwargs[\\'align_time\\']] + kwargs[\\'time_window\\'][0],\\n            trials_df[kwargs[\\'align_time\\']] + kwargs[\\'time_window\\'][1]\\n        ]).T\\n        chunk_len = kwargs[\\'time_window\\'][1] - kwargs[\\'time_window\\'][0]\\n        interval_len = (\\n            kwargs[\\'time_window\\'][1] - kwargs[\\'time_window\\'][0]\\n        )\\n    else:\\n        assert intervals is not None, \\'Require intervals to segment the recording into chunks including trials and non-trials.\\'\\n        chunk_len = intervals[0,1] - intervals[0,0]\\n        interval_len = (\\n            intervals[0,1] - intervals[0,0]\\n        )\\n\\n    # subselect spikes for this region\\n    spikemask = np.isin(neural_df[\\'spike_clusters\\'], reg_clu_ids)\\n    regspikes = neural_df[\\'spike_times\\'][spikemask]\\n    regclu = neural_df[\\'spike_clusters\\'][spikemask]\\n    clusters_used_in_bins = np.unique(regclu)\\n\\n    binsize = kwargs.get(\\'binsize\\', chunk_len)\\n    \\n    if chunk_len / binsize == 1.0:\\n        # one vector of neural activity per interval\\n        binned, _ = get_spike_counts_in_bins(regspikes, regclu, intervals)\\n        binned = binned.T  # binned is a 2D array\\n        binned_list = [x[None, :] for x in binned]\\n    else:\\n        binned_array = get_spike_data_per_interval(\\n            regspikes, regclu,\\n            interval_begs=intervals[:, 0],\\n            interval_ends=intervals[:, 1],\\n            interval_len=interval_len,\\n            binsize=kwargs[\\'binsize\\'],\\n            n_workers=n_workers)\\n        binned_list = [x.T for x in binned_array]   \\n    return np.array(binned_list), clusters_used_in_bins\\n\\n\\ndef cache staging ---\\n#!/bin/bash\\n#\\n# Assemble the ONE cache at /app/data/one_cache from the read-only dataset mount.\\n#\\n# The dataset is shared across trials and jobs and must not be written, but ONE writes\\n# while it reads: it caches Alyx REST responses under .rest, and refreshes the release\\n# tables. Mounting the whole cache read-only makes those writes fail. So the session data\\n# is symlinked to the read-only mount, and the small things ONE writes are copied in and\\n# stay writable. /app/data/one_cache then behaves like an ordinary ONE cache.\\n#\\n# Mounting the dataset writable instead would cost three things: rootless podman would\\n# be required, because the datasets live on Kerberos-squashed NFS where a docker\\n# container runs as squashed root and the first write fails; concurrent trials would\\n# write .rest into one shared tree; and an agent could modify or delete the dataset.\\n#\\n# A download into a symlinked lab directory still fails, which is intended: the fetch is\\n# complete, and a trial has no business pulling data over the network.\\n#\\n# Runs as the image ENTRYPOINT, so it happens once per container before anything else and\\n# then hands over to whatever command was supplied.\\nset -eu\\n\\nSRC=/mnt/dataset\\nDST=/app/data\\n\\nif [ -d \"$SRC/one_cache\" ]; then\\n    mkdir -p \"$DST/one_cache\"\\n\\n    for path in \"$SRC\"/one_cache/*/; do\\n        name=$(basename \"$path\")\\n        case \"$name\" in\\n            # Release tables, ~7 MB. Copied rather than symlinked so that load_cache()\\n            # can refresh them in place.\\n            Brainwidemap|*_IBL_et_al_*)\\n                cp -r \"$path\" \"$DST/one_cache/$name\"\\n                ;;\\n            # Session data, hundreds of GB. Symlinked; this is what must stay read-only.\\n            *)\\n                ln -sfn \"${path%/}\" \"$DST/one_cache/$name\"\\n                ;;\\n        esac\\n    done\\n\\n    # Cached Alyx REST responses: 6462 of them on the full dataset, 171 on the subset.\\n    # Copied, not symlinked: an agent\\'s queries will not be the reference\\'s, and caching\\n    # a new response into a read-only directory would fail.\\n    if [ -d \"$SRC/one_cache/.rest\" ]; then\\n        cp -r \"$SRC/one_cache/.rest\" \"$DST/one_cache/.rest\"\\n    fi\\n\\n    # Only the datalimit dataset ships this; convert_data.py filters to it when present\\n    # and processes everything when it is absent, which is how one solution file serves\\n    # both the full and the reduced dataset.\\n    if [ -f \"$SRC/DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv\" ]; then\\n        cp \"$SRC/DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv\" \"$DST/DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv\"\\n    fi\\n\\n    # ONE\\'s parameters, holding the Alyx auth token. Building a client with a password\\n    # re-authenticates over the network; with this in place it does not connect at all.\\n    # The filename is the one ONE derives from the database URL -- it reads parameters\\n    # from $HOME/.one, never from the cache directory, so leaving the file beside the\\n    # data achieves nothing on its own. Without this step every session fails with\\n    #   \\'Alyx authentication failed with credentials: user = intbrainlab, password = None\\'\\n    # and the conversion writes an empty dataset without reporting an error.\\n    if [ -f \"$SRC/one_params.json\" ]; then\\n        mkdir -p \"$HOME/.one\"\\n        cp \"$SRC/one_params.json\" \"$HOME/.one/.openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org\"\\n\\n        # .caches is read FIRST by one.params.get(), and the token file alone is not\\n        # enough (ONE 3.5.2, one/params.py::get):\\n        #\\n        #     cache_map = iopar.read(f\\'{_PAR_ID_STR}/{_CLIENT_ID_STR}\\', {})\\n        #     if not cache_map or client_key not in cache_map.CLIENT_MAP:\\n        #         cache_map = setup(client=client, silent=silent, ...)\\n        #\\n        # With no .caches, setup() runs and rewrites the parameters from defaults,\\n        # DISCARDING the TOKEN it just copied and pointing CACHE_DIR at\\n        # ~/Downloads/ONE. The client is then built with no token and no password, and\\n        # because silent=True only warns, every session fails with\\n        #   \\'Alyx authentication failed with credentials: user = intbrainlab,\\n        #    password = None\\'\\n        # while the run still exits successfully. With this file in place the loaded\\n        # parameters keep their TOKEN and CACHE_DIR points at the staged cache.\\n        cat > \"$HOME/.one/.caches\" <<EOF\\n{\"CLIENT_MAP\": {\"openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org\": \"$DST/one_cache\"},\\n \"DEFAULT\": \"openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org\"}\\nEOF\\n    fi\\nfi\\n\\nexec \"$@\"\\n'}]\n\nSTEP 18\nOBS: [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\\nWall time 0.1 seconds\\nOutput:\\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'pypdf ERR No module named \\'pypdf\\'\\nPyPDF2 ERR No module named \\'PyPDF2\\'\\nfitz ERR No module named \\'fitz\\'\\npdfplumber ERR No module named \\'pdfplumber\\'\\none ok 3.5.2\\nbrainbox ok \\niblatlas ok 1.2.0\\n            intervals[0,1] - intervals[0,0]\\n        )\\n\\n    # subselect spikes for this region\\n    spikemask = np.isin(neural_df[\\'spike_clusters\\'], reg_clu_ids)\\n    regspikes = neural_df[\\'spike_times\\'][spikemask]\\n    regclu = neural_df[\\'spike_clusters\\'][spikemask]\\n    clusters_used_in_bins = np.unique(regclu)\\n\\n    binsize = kwargs.get(\\'binsize\\', chunk_len)\\n    \\n    if chunk_len / binsize == 1.0:\\n        # one vector of neural activity per interval\\n        binned, _ = get_spike_counts_in_bins(regspikes, regclu, intervals)\\n        binned = binned.T  # binned is a 2D array\\n        binned_list = [x[None, :] for x in binned]\\n    else:\\n        binned_array = get_spike_data_per_interval(\\n            regspikes, regclu,\\n            interval_begs=intervals[:, 0],\\n            interval_ends=intervals[:, 1],\\n            interval_len=interval_len,\\n            binsize=kwargs[\\'binsize\\'],\\n            n_workers=n_workers)\\n        binned_list = [x.T for x in binned_array]   \\n    return np.array(binned_list), clusters_used_in_bins\\n\\n\\ndef load_target_behavior(one, eid, target):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Parameters\\n    ----------\\n    target : str\\n        \\'wheel-position\\' | \\'wheel-velocity\\' | \\'wheel-speed\\' | \\n        \\'left-whisker-motion-energy\\' | \\'right-whisker-motion-energy\\' | \\n        \\'left-pupil-diameter\\' | \\'right-pupil-diameter\\' |\\n        \\'left-camera-left-paw-speed\\' | \\'left-camera-right-paw-speed\\' | \\n        \\'right-camera-left-paw-speed\\' | \\'right-camera-right-paw-speed\\' |\\n        \\'left-nose-speed\\' | \\'right-nose-speed\\'\\n    one : \\n    eid : str\\n\\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n    dict\\n        \\'times\\': timestamps for behavior signal\\n        \\'values\\': associated values\\n        \\'skip\\': bool, True if there was an error loading data\\n    \"\"\"\\n\\n    # To load wheel and motion energy, we just use the SessionLoader, e.g.\\n    sess_loader = SessionLoader(one, eid)\\n    \\n    # wheel is a dataframe that contains wheel times and position interpolated to a uniform sampling rate, velocity and\\n    # acceleration computed using Gaussian smoothing\\n    try:\\n        if target == \\'wheel-position\\':\\n            sess_loader.load_wheel()\\n            beh_dict = {\\n                \\'times\\': sess_loader.wheel[\\'times\\'].to_numpy(),\\n                \\'values\\': sess_loader.wheel[\\'position\\'].to_numpy()\\n            }\\n        elif target == \\'wheel-velocity\\':\\n            sess_loader.load_wheel()\\n            beh_dict = {\\n                \\'times\\': sess_loader.wheel[\\'times\\'].to_numpy(),\\n                \\'values\\': sess_loader.wheel[\\'velocity\\'].to_numpy()\\n            }\\n        elif target == \\'wheel-speed\\':\\n            sess_loader.load_wheel()\\n            beh_dict = {\\n                \\'times\\': sess_loader.wheel[\\'times\\'].to_numpy(),\\n                \\'values\\': np.abs(sess_loader.wheel[\\'velocity\\'].to_numpy())\\n            }\\n        # motion_energy is a dictionary of dataframes, each containing the times and the motion energy for each view\\n        # for the side views, they contain columns [\\'times\\', \\'whiskerMotionEnergy\\'] for the body view it contains\\n        # [\\'times\\', \\'bodyMotionEnergy\\']\\n        elif target == \\'left-whisker-motion-energy\\':\\n            sess_loader.load_motion_energy(views=[\\'left\\'])\\n            beh_dict = {\\n                \\'times\\': sess_loader.motion_energy[\\'leftCamera\\'][\\'times\\'].to_numpy(),\\n                \\'values\\': sess_loader.motion_energy[\\'leftCamera\\'][\\'whiskerMotionEnergy\\'].to_numpy()\\n            }\\n        elif target == \\'right-whisker-motion-energy\\':\\n            sess_loader.load_motion_energy(views=[\\'right\\'])\\n            beh_dict = {\\n                \\'times\\': sess_loader.motion_energy[\\'rightCamera\\'][\\'times\\'].to_numpy(),\\n                \\'values\\': sess_loader.motion_energy[\\'rightCamera\\'][\\'whiskerMotionEnergy\\'].to_numpy()\\n            }\\n        elif target == \\'left-pupil-diameter\\':\\n            dlc_left = one.load_object(eid, \"leftCamera\", attribute=[\"dlc\", \"features\", \"times\"], collection=\"alf\")\\n            beh_dict = {\\n                \\'times\\': dlc_left.times,\\n                \\'values\\': dlc_left.features.pupilDiameter_smooth\\n            }\\n        elif target == \\'right-pupil-diameter\\':\\n            dlc_right = one.load_object(eid, \"rightCamera\", attribute=[\"dlc\", \"features\", \"times\"], collection=\"alf\")\\n            beh_dict = {\\n                \\'times\\': dlc_right.times,\\n                \\'values\\': dlc_right.features.pupilDiameter_smooth\\n            }\\n        elif target == \\'left-camera-left-paw-speed\\':\\n            dlc_left = one.load_object(eid, \"leftCamera\", attribute=[\"dlc\",
